           My definition and understanding of CP (compression):

           CP is price returning to the last minor SD (supply/demand) or DP (decision point) to
           consume any remaining pockets of orders that are left, so that when the institutions
           place their big orders at the larger SD zone, they can easily drive price back to the base
           of the CP, or further towards the origin of the move.

           DocS

           The following user(s) said Thank You: extremerrage, Mrgerry, bobyv87, naascott, Alexander

           Thanks 2oz.

           CPs 13 to 19:

           My thoughts on these CPs is that they are really flags in a trend. They allow momentum
           to build up for a further push higher. These shallow CP flags can allow a trend channel
           to continue for quite some time as price doesn't get overstretched. They can provide an
           opportunity to join a move that is already under way. I guess the CP that is really of

https://www.readthemarket.com/index.php/en/forum/journals/1640-drswing-on-course                              9/27
7/27/2018                             DrSwing on course - RTM Community

           value is where price compresses into a strong SD zone, so you get in at the start of a

                 HOME   EDUCATION     FORUM                                       MORE  LOGOUT
           move with the Big Boys and a chance of good R:R. The CP into the zone can be an

           indication that the SD zone will be a strong level.

           Please do challenge me on any thoughts that are inaccurate .

           DocS

         Quasimodo:    EDUCATION     FORUM                                        MORE            LOGOUT

OK I feel ready to move on to the next learning stage, which will be seeking out the
QM(Quasimodo). I will also look to carry on with CP by observing how it combines with
the QM.

I will look for HTF SD zones and then drill down to the LTF to spot the QM at or in those
zones.

My definition of a QM:

A QM occurs at a swing point in the market, in or around an SD zone. A bullish QM
would have a L(low), a H(high), a LL(lower low), a HH(higher high) and then a
HL(higher low) occurring at an SD zone. This pattern can happen all in the one phase
or over a longer period of time. Chart examples to follow.

           Hi Dr. Swing
           I just came across this post where you mentioned a trade which I took.
           You are referring to it as QM, which I wont say is wrong since there is no precise
           definition of QM, but some purists might argue that if L on left shoulder is violated by a
           lower L on the right side, it wont be a valid QM.
           I would refer to these trades as engulfing trades.
           IF also recently posted a statement about engulfing trades in classroom section.
           Good luck and keep up with good work.

           The following user(s) said Thank You: DrSwing, cuimingzhao

                        DrSwing                Offline Master Trader

                                           Posts: 419 Thank you received: 1084

           Hi Mike, lots of different techniques here on RTM for entering trades, and you will end
           up using which ever is comfortable for you. As this is an M5 chart you don't need to dig
           any deeper, as the zone is very narrow and provides good R:R if you TT the edge. As
           you can see PA also showed up in the zone, if your preference is to wait on it. My
           preference here would have been TT trades.

https://www.readthemarket.com/index.php/en/forum/journals/1640-drswing-on-course?start=250                      2/38
7/27/2018                             DrSwing on course - Page 11 - RTM Community

           EDIT:
           I should point out that when we talk about PA here on RTM, we are talking about
           engulfs of zones, and not individual candle PA, but many here do use candle PA.
           However I believe IF would say to forget the candles and concentrate on the zones :) .

           Red Knight                Offline Trader                                               Posts: 91

                              Thank you received: 74

           Hi,
           As Pedini said'first to understand how price move etc.
           Stage1
           IF made for this video (Not sure name of the video)He explain on instrument -GOLD
           how price move and why price move(just search in IF video section.
           2.Harry in markepedia post where he explain in details FL and FTR concepts
           3.Go to Pedini thread and you will find his explain in details PAZ concept+many
           FL+FTR examples.Start to read from 2013 and sit in his journal and try to understand
           each his trade.
           After done all this you will have done build basic fundation for next step which is:
           Stage2
           Go to the IF videos again and find his video about (reading session)then do homework
           and apply what you had learn already +you will find some new things and put this all
           together with notes.
           Stage-3
           When you done that go to the classroom I think page 12 and find IF quiz with questions
           and read all answears specially (Benhur's and Dr.Swing+IF's) after this exercise you
           will get instructions how to make trading plan for trade before trade and also how you
           should manage trade after entry.
           Final stage:
           After all this you should have your own trading method if not go back to stage-1.
           When you've done that open again your MT4 and you are ready to practice on demo
           acc.all what you learn.
           When you can make money consistently on demo acc. open real acc. but if you can not
           make consistently money on demo you have two options:

https://www.readthemarket.com/index.php/en/forum/journals/1640-drswing-on-course?start=250                                  7/38
7/27/2018                                   DrSwing on course - Page 11 - RTM Community

           1,Practice harder

           HOME               EDUCATION     FORUM                                           MORE  LOGOUT
           2.Forget about trading and try something else.

           P.s.It is not about how many hours you will spend learn or about screen time.It is about
           how you understand all and how you will put all together. So don't count hrs and don't
           only concentrate watching charts.You need to be active(be teacher and student in the
           same time).You need to find your own edge in the market .There is not teacher who
           give you this.Personally I am not using in trading words'easy' 'simple' 'short period of
           learn'

           For me there is not only learn one thing.Understand each concept is easy but harder
           part is put all together and make on it money.
           If all this is too much mess here is one tip:
           Start with every IF post and videos then do the same process with Harry's and
           Pedini's.Only watching and reading their posts is not enough you need to study deep
           and understand it(all this is the simple part of you trading journey b/c they explain all in
           very understandable way even if English is not you mother language).After done all that
           I am guarantee you will know what you need to do next.
           First goal is understand concepts.
           Second goal is consistently making money on your knowledge(Reaching first goal will
           not automatically guarantee reaching second goal specially in trading bussines) .

           All the best.
           RK

                        DrSwing                Offline Master Trader

                                           Posts: 419 Thank you received: 1084

           H, In my view not all demand orders need to get removed in CP, just the larger pockets,
           to ease the move back down through it. Also you will notice that if you check A1 & A2
           areas, marked on the chart below in a LTF, you can see more evidence of demand
           removal on the way up.

           When price returns to the CP zone, these tiny pockets of demand still cause a reaction,
           but the small numbers of buyers here are easily overcome by the sellers from the flip
           area of B above. Unless B gets engulfed north the sellers are in control. Once the
           bottom edge of the PAZ is engulfed south we are free to move into the zone below.

Hotochin wrote:
Thanks DR. I followed everything you said perfectly. I am studying the PDF on
CP to make sure I understand that concept. Some of the grey areas you marked
below don't make sense to me.

Thanks,
H

H, the grey areas are just my rough guide to show how the small pockets of demand in
the CP on the left caused a reaction on the right, but there was not enough buy orders
to push price up through the stronger supply from B above.

Now moving on from that, to my mind there was quite a lot of activity as price was
dropping here, and the supply at B has been tapped a couple of times, so maybe there
is now actually some accumulation going on here. The spike into the lower zone would
have triggered some stops, so the ITs may be looking to take price higher now to a
stronger supply area for an eventual move lower.

The ITs need to play games with price so that they can fill their large orders, and it is

           Hi sylntthunder, It's difficult to know what to say to you. I try to think like an IT and look
           to where large pockets of LQ may be sitting. This level looked obvious to me because
           of the fast move away from it previously. If you then have supply 30 - 40 pips beyond
           the zone then it makes for a good FO level. You just need to go painstakingly through
           the charts and observe how levels behaved in the past. Hope this helps some.

           above post, and thus the possibility of an FO, so I stayed extra alert. Price made 2
           bearish pins but still didn't drop, and even put in a counter bullish pin, so I decided that
           an FO was probably on the cards.

           H, the 2 yellow arrows point to FLs (DPs), and as these resolved south, then there is
           supply there.

                        DrSwing                             Offline Master Trader

                        Posts: 419 Thank you received: 1084

           Well guys and gals it's time for me to fess up!

           I'll be a year here on RTM next week, and for months now I can smell and even touch
           the other side, but I just cant break through. Right now I find I am spinning my wheels,
           basically going around in circles, in frustration. So very often I have felt so close to
           being at one with the markets, only to awake the following day a stranger again. I am
           not learning anything new and not having any new aha moments, and this week in
           particular I just seem to have a mental block. Sure I can read the past PA all day and all
           night, but I just can't trade the hard right edge! Not even on Forextester!

           When I first joined I posted quite a lot, but then I found that the more I learned the more
           difficult it became to post. You see, I wanted to be able to help lead the way, but I found
           that my posting was often inaccurate and therefore misleading to members. So I would
           only post the things that I was definite about. This made it very difficult for me to make
           comments on posts or attempt to answer members questions. So I worked harder in
           silence, digging into the charts in order to be able to make posts that would add value
           to the forum.

           Now heading into my second year, I feel something needs to change. My plan is to start
           posting regularly again with you all clearly knowing that I am just a mickey mouse trader

https://www.readthemarket.com/index.php/en/forum/journals/1640-drswing-on-course?start=275                      29/35
7/27/2018                                  DrSwing on course - Page 12 - RTM Community

           who is still learning. Even if my posts don't help anyone maybe it will at least help shift

           HOME           EDUCATION        FORUM                                            MORE  LOGOUT
           my own mental block. Maybe I need the forum now more than it needs me. So please

           be aware that I am further from being a master trader than most of you posting here,

           and that everything I post is just my own probably misguided understanding.

           Thank you If for your continued support and your patience with our silence.

           God Bless all
           drswing

           Warren           Offline Banned                                                               Posts: 355

           Dr Swing,

           The last part of the Journey always seems the hardest. We are battle worn, tired and
           feel vulnerable.

           That overwhelming urge to come "full circle" with any of our endeavours is what keeps
           us going. I know your frustration , It is like a splinter in your mind. The hardest thing is
           to accept is we may not be there yet, where others think we are. Being put on a
           pedestal is like being judged on every post you make. The most important thing you
           want to give to any reader is the correct information, this really exposes you in the raw.
           You are right or wrong , simple as that.

           It is one thing for the market to prove you wrong , but it is another to make it public too.

           As traders we must accept that until we master ourselves , we can not master the
           market.

           you can be technically right 100% at chart reading. But what about your state of mind at
           that time, are there any life distractions, do you feel under pressure to perform. Have
           you had an argument with a loved one, is your boss winding you up.

           The further up life's ladder you go , the more pressure to perform is on your shoulders.

           All the professionals I have ever known , still spend countless hours going over the
           basics. EVEN those at the top of there game.

           I have found the best way to deal with my demons is to face them FULL on. With more
           persistence and energy than before.

https://www.readthemarket.com/index.php/en/forum/journals/1640-drswing-on-course?start=300                           3/35
7/27/2018                               DrSwing on course - Page 13 - RTM Community

           moments can be the most enlightening.

           DrSwing on course                                                                              #303

                        DrSwing                          Offline Master Trader

                        Posts: 419 Thank you received: 1084

           Ok, this post is an insight into one method of how I try to find my trade opps.

           I read the charts to find zone engulfs rather than just DBR/RBD engulfs as this gives
           the desired R:R.

           I see the charts in terms of Poles and Flags and I'm looking to the SR transition level
           between the PAZs. This trade level has to be an M1 SD zone 3-4 pips wide and
           providing an FO to the above/below FTR/FL. I then trade the return to this level, giving
           me a potentially massive R:R. Some pics below to paint this picture.

https://www.readthemarket.com/index.php/en/forum/journals/1640-drswing-on-course?start=300                      4/35
7/27/2018                                DrSwing on course - Page 13 - RTM Community

           This would be my main view.

           HOME  EDUCATION               FORUM                                              MORE  LOGOUT

DrSwing wrote: Well guys and gals it's time for me to fess up!

I'll be a year here on RTM next week, and for months now I can smell and even
touch the other side, but I just cant break through. Right now I find I am spinning
my wheels, basically going around in circles, in frustration. So very often I have
felt so close to being at one with the markets, only to awake the following day a
stranger again. I am not learning anything new and not having any new aha
moments, and this week in particular I just seem to have a mental block. Sure I

https://www.readthemarket.com/index.php/en/forum/journals/1640-drswing-on-course?start=300                          8/35
7/27/2018                                  DrSwing on course - Page 13 - RTM Community

           can read the past PA all day and all night, but I just can't trade the hard right
           HOME           EDUCATION        FORUM                                            MORE  LOGOUT
           edge! Not even on Forextester!

           When I first joined I posted quite a lot, but then I found that the more I learned
           the more difficult it became to post. You see, I wanted to be able to help lead the
           way, but I found that my posting was often inaccurate and therefore misleading
           to members. So I would only post the things that I was definite about. This made
           it very difficult for me to make comments on posts or attempt to answer members
           questions. So I worked harder in silence, digging into the charts in order to be
           able to make posts that would add value to the forum.

           Now heading into my second year, I feel something needs to change. My plan is
           to start posting regularly again with you all clearly knowing that I am just a
           mickey mouse trader who is still learning. Even if my posts don't help anyone
           maybe it will at least help shift my own mental block. Maybe I need the forum
           now more than it needs me. So please be aware that I am further from being a
           master trader than most of you posting here, and that everything I post is just my
           own probably misguided understanding.

           Thank you If for your continued support and your patience with our silence.

           God Bless all
           drswing

Ok, this post is an insight into one method of how I try to find my trade opps.

         I read the charts to find zone engulfs rather than just DBR/RBD engulfs as this gives        10/35

https://www.readthemarket.com/index.php/en/forum/journals/1640-drswing-on-course?start=300
7/27/2018                    DrSwing on course - Page 13 - RTM Community

         the desired R:R.  EDUCATION  FORUM                                                 MORE  LOGOUT

                 HOME

I see the charts in terms of Poles and Flags and I'm looking to the SR transition level
between the PAZs. This trade level has to be an M1 SD zone 3-4 pips wide and
providing an FO to the above/below FTR/FL. I then trade the return to this level, giving
me a potentially massive R:R. Some pics below to paint this picture.

This would be my main view.

I then draw the SR level.

Look for the FTR that needs to be FOed and the FO level.

This shows how I narrow down the FO zone.

Trade level selected.

         Notice how my selected SD level lines up on the left.                                            11/35

Ok, this post is an insight into one method of how I try to find my trade opps.

I read the charts to find zone engulfs rather than just DBR/RBD engulfs as this gives
the desired R:R.

I see the charts in terms of Poles and Flags and I'm looking to the SR transition level
between the PAZs. This trade level has to be an M1 SD zone 3-4 pips wide and
providing an FO to the above/below FTR/FL. I then trade the return to this level, giving

         Hi Rex, sure, I will get to that in a following post, but first this :)                          23/35

https://www.readthemarket.com/index.php/en/forum/journals/1640-drswing-on-course?start=300
7/27/2018                             DrSwing on course - Page 13 - RTM Community

           Something struck me in a post from Hotrod this morning about himself.

           HOME         EDUCATION     FORUM                                                 MORE  LOGOUT

           HotRod wrote: .................. I need to loosen up my thought process on price and
           FTRs. I am still wanting price to have defined rigid rules and then obey those
           rules.......................

           My understanding is that price is not a monthly, weekly, or daily event that happens the
           same on each occasion. Price is a story that unfolds through time, and each event
           plays out differently on each occasion as price adapts to it's surroundings. This is why
           they say that understanding the markets is more of an art than a science. So once you
           understand all the elements of price reading (the science) that are thought here in RTM,
           you then use that science to decipher (the art) the unfolding story. The story is always
           about the ITs moving price from source to destination while playing tricks to hide the
           route they are taking from the reader. This is why the often double back and set traps to
           cover their tracks and confuse the tracker. They will not use the identical paths and
           identical tricks on each occasion.

           What I am trying to put accross here, is that price events can be similar, but seldom the
           same. This is why Hotrod's statement is so important to grasp.

           The following user(s) said Thank You: bulbuli, Rakkain, Al3cs

           Rex, this is just my own way of working right now and will possibly change over time.

           On the first chart the boxes are just roughtly drawn as a visual reference. You could
           also draw boxes within the boxes but these would give smaller moves and reduced
           R:R. Really is just my way of seeing poles and flags for now. I then look for a pole
           connecting the boxes and find the FL as shown in chart 2 below.

https://www.readthemarket.com/index.php/en/forum/journals/1640-drswing-on-course?start=300                25/35

                              Though we have all come to trading to make "handy"
                              money, my advice to beginners is to look for the
                              understanding first, then the money will follow. Trade
                              your understanding and it will give you natural entries
                              and exits. If you have to look too hard to find a trade
                              instead of the trade finding you, then you have to
                              keep working on your understanding.

           Hi scoobydo, I am delighted you have found my journal of use on you own journey to
           trading enlightenment.

           I am currently honing my TT skills, as I want to trade If-like! . I will TT my level if I
           can secure a tight stoploss, other wise I wait for PA as this can give a small sl with
           conformation.

           My advice to anyone new would be to trade PA until you have mastered that method.
           All my levels are found on the far left of the chart. To be confident in your levels you
           need to dig into the charts to prove to yourself what levels work. Aaron has been an
           inspirational example of posting his work on the forum. It takes alot of back testing and
           chart digging down to M1 level. Confidence in your levels will come down to your own
           chart work. Nothing anyone can tell you will take the place of your own chart study. If
           you need an answer to a question that can be found by chart study, then dig in and find
           it for yourself, as it is the best learning. Your own chart study will bring you many aha!
           monents.

           All this advice is from my own experience. Enjoy the journey .

           The following user(s) said Thank You: scoobydoo, cuimingzhao

           DrSwing wrote: Well guys and gals it's time for me to fess up!

           I'll be a year here on RTM next week, and for months now I can smell and even
           touch the other side, but I just cant break through. Right now I find I am spinning
           my wheels, basically going around in circles, in frustration. So very often I have
           felt so close to being at one with the markets, only to awake the following day a
           stranger again. I am not learning anything new and not having any new aha
           moments, and this week in particular I just seem to have a mental block. Sure I
           can read the past PA all day and all night, but I just can't trade the hard right
           edge! Not even on Forextester!

           When I first joined I posted quite a lot, but then I found that the more I learned
           the more difficult it became to post. You see, I wanted to be able to help lead the
           way, but I found that my posting was often inaccurate and therefore misleading
           to members. So I would only post the things that I was definite about. This made
           it very difficult for me to make comments on posts or attempt to answer members
           questions. So I worked harder in silence, digging into the charts in order to be
           able to make posts that would add value to the forum.

           Now heading into my second year, I feel something needs to change. My plan is

           to start posting regularly again with you all clearly knowing that I am just a

           mickey mouse trader who is still learning. Even if my posts don't help anyone

           maybe it will at least help shift my own mental block. Maybe I need the forum

           now more than it needs me. So please be aware that I am further from being a

https://www.readthemarket.com/index.php/en/forum/journals/1640-drswing-on-course?start=350                14/35
7/27/2018                                         DrSwing on course - Page 15 - RTM Community

           master trader than most of you posting here, and that everything I post is just my
           HOME           EDUCATION               FORUM  MORE                                  LOGOUT

           Thank you If for your continued support and your patience with our silence.

           God Bless all
           drswing

           I will be posting less trades going forward, as I will be moving towards more quality
           swing type trades, rather that the SAS I have been practicising of late. There should be

7/27/2018                DrSwing on course - Page 16 - RTM Community

           I would encourage all to start and maintain a journal if you are not doing so already. It

           will help your learning and also leave nuggets behind for others to draw from. You

           never know which of your posts will set some trader on that AHA! trail. Many times it is

           just how YOU express or draw something in a post or on a chart, that makes the

           difference from the same thing that has been said a hundred times before!

           I wrote this in my very first post: " As the great Zig Zigler wrote " You'll get what you
           want when you've helped enough people get what they want"."

           This one below was easy to see and take!

           joema                                Offline Trader                                    Posts: 157

                              Thank you received: 68

           DrSwing wrote: Right third time lucky :)

           Ok, this post is an insight into one method of how I try to find my trade opps.

           I read the charts to find zone engulfs rather than just DBR/RBD engulfs as this
           gives the desired R:R.

           I see the charts in terms of Poles and Flags and I'm looking to the SR transition
           level between the PAZs. This trade level has to be an M1 SD zone 3-4 pips wide
           and providing an FO to the above/below FTR/FL. I then trade the return to this
           level, giving me a potentially massive R:R. Some pics below to paint this picture.

           This would be my main view

https://www.readthemarket.com/index.php/en/forum/journals/1640-drswing-on-course?start=375                      28/39
7/27/2018                             DrSwing on course - Page 16 - RTM Community

           HOME  EDUCATION            FORUM                                                 MORE  LOGOUT

           I then draw the SR level.

7/27/2018        DrSwing on course - Page 16 - RTM Community

polymorph wrote: Are the gray boxes labeled 1,2,3, and 4 your targets, DrS? Or
are you just planning to ride it down and let PA take you out?

The lighter color boxes are decisions I'm always a bit confused by, either with
entry, exit, or just what to expect in general. At each one, price comes back and
deeply retraces the base - does that weaken it or is it still a strong point given
how price left? Since orders generated the move up, there must have still been a
lot of buys to move price that far that quickly, but some of them had to be

https://www.readthemarket.com/index.php/en/forum/journals/1640-drswing-on-course?start=400                      3/32
7/27/2018                               DrSwing on course - Page 17 - RTM Community

           absorbed by the amount of time price spent there too.
           HOME         EDUCATION       FORUM                     MORE                      LOGOUT

           Your ideas are appreciated but I think this is something I need to spend some
           time on too.

           EDIT: I just realized that 2 is different than 3 or 4. At 2, price hadn't closed
           outside of the base whereas at 3 and 4 it left and came back. So at current I
           think 1 is stronger than 2 based on the time spent there, and 2 is stronger than 3
           or 4 due to FTB having already occurred. Thoughts?

           Yes to my mind everytime a level is touched it is weakened, that's why we are always
           encouraged to trade fresh levels. No matter how price moves away from a level, all
           levels need to be put in context. When running a trade I look for the nearest HTF SD
           level and then watch PA on the LTFs at that level. I would be watching PA at your No.1
           level. Always keep in mind where price might ultimately want to go.

           The following user(s) said Thank You: pauga, cuimingzhao

           DrSwing wrote: Right third time lucky :)

           Ok, this post is an insight into one method of how I try to find my trade opps.

           I read the charts to find zone engulfs rather than just DBR/RBD engulfs as this
           gives the desired R:R.

           I see the charts in terms of Poles and Flags and I'm looking to the SR transition
           level between the PAZs. This trade level has to be an M1 SD zone 3-4 pips wide
           and providing an FO to the above/below FTR/FL. I then trade the return to this
           level, giving me a potentially massive R:R. Some pics below to paint this picture.

           This would be my main view

https://www.readthemarket.com/index.php/en/forum/journals/1640-drswing-on-course?start=400                        22/32
7/27/2018                             DrSwing on course - Page 17 - RTM Community

           HOME  EDUCATION            FORUM                                                 MORE  LOGOUT

           Look for the FTR that needs to be FOed and the FO level.

           Gigiocean, I'm not sure exactly how this can be explained to your understanding. The
           ITs are always looking to trick us by FOing levels to hit stops and BOs, to provide the
           LQ they need to fill their large orders. The FO needs to be into a pocket of S/D. Here is
           a trade I am currently in. This needed alot of patience, something that is a work in
           progress for me.

           polymorph                                          Offline Trader                              Posts: 133

                                                 Thank you received: 231

           Maybe what's being missed is the power of a 2.5 pip zone plus spread as a stop - on
           the UJ trade that's only about a 3.5-4 pip SL. If you entered at the lower zone and
           factored the FO into your SL, you're in at a far worse price due to the larger stop and
           hence your reward suffers. That seems like a really good incentive to hunt for the FO!

           I don't think the difference between 10x+ vs. 2x, 4x, or even 6x trades can be
           overstated, especially over a series of many trades.

           The following user(s) said Thank You: cuimingzhao

           polymorph                                   Offline Trader                             Posts: 133

                                                 Thank you received: 231

           Very nice trade, prime! I'm obviously not DrS, but I've seen If (and it seems like DrS too)
           say 2 things on the subject: 1. get out where you'd get in going the other direction (so in
           this case, close your sell where you'd buy); 2. let PA take you out (so let an ENG or
           other movement in the opposite direction of your trade tell you it's time to close.)

           Again, that's a really nice, patient trade you've got there, so nothing to be disappointed
           with even if the down move continues!

           of your trade tell you it's time to close.)

           With all the new talent posting in RTM, I find it a very very exciting place right now
                                 . SO so so many good posts, charts and trades by so many, for all to

           learn!!!

           I started my journal here for the selfish reason of helping myself, to develop my trading.
           I then found that by recording my understanding of what RTM was teaching in my
           journal, was also helping others on their journey, and answering questions for others in
           the threads helped my understanding of concepts much better. Sure, all the information
           needed is already there in other journals, but it is often how we express something that
           makes the difference to another's understanding.

           I continue to post because I owe it to If for freely starting RTM, and to the likes of Harry,
           Benhur, Pedini, Les_Paul, Kruger and many others who have helped make this

           PASniper                                                Offline Trader                 Posts: 36

                                              Thank you received: 40

           milkeyway wrote: Hi Dr. Swing or any other expert. Can you please tell me why
           price did not respect this fresh supply ? Thanks.

           Hi MW, I am not any expert, but you need to remember:

           "So here's what we look for on the charts:

           General: HTF. Know where price is coming from and going to, and the PA past and
           present in all the TFs, from the Monthly down.

           Specific: At the zones you want to trade, look to

           Past. study the zone in all TFs, down to M1

https://www.readthemarket.com/index.php/en/forum/journals/1640-drswing-on-course?start=450                      3/31
7/27/2018                           DrSwing on course - Page 19 - RTM Community

           ask yourself

           HOME             EDUCATION  FORUM                                                MORE  LOGOUT
           Where were the decisions made? Clean S/D? Mark these lines. No clean S/D? -

           compressed zone

           Did price really shoot away from the zone, or did it CP away?

           Did the zone itself react at the right place? Look beyond the zone further into the past.

           See what it reacted to. Was there a better S/D nearby that price wants to visit? This

           explains many fakeouts.

           Present.
           Approach.
           How is price returning to the zone?
           Where's the nearest flag in the TF you want to trade? This is your tg1 in this TF. Flags
           in the LTFs? What does PA tell you?
           Has price tested the last flag on approach? (good sign)
           Has price compressed into the zone in this TF or LTFs? (good sign)
           Is there big news on the way? Has there just been big news?

           Reaction
           In LTF, does price react violently to the first decision point? Does it quickly engulf the
           nearest S/D? (good sign)
           Does price simply CP away? Maybe it wants to go to the next decision point
           If the first decision point breaks, watch the signs on approach to the next, and, of
           course, reaction.

           Chew this over for now. Apply it to your chart history. Apply it to as many failed setups
           as successful ones. Millions of them if possible! Capture and file them all. This will help
           make it instinctive."

           Supply and demand are not enough reasons to enter trades, watch how price arrives to
           a level (Compression, 3D...), if no PA - there is no trade; then wait for LTF PA (DM, QM,
           CanCan) to enter. Use TT only at DPs at levels where there is a high probability trade
           (previous QM level, FOs, ignored levels.

           The following user(s) said Thank You: strudl, KGB, cuimingzhao

https://www.readthemarket.com/index.php/en/forum/journals/1640-drswing-on-course?start=450                4/31
7/27/2018                                             DrSwing on course - Page 19 - RTM Community

           HOME         EDUCATION                     FORUM                                 MORE   LOGOUT

           "Trabajar para ganarse la vida esta bien. Pero, ¿Por qué esa vida que uno se gana tiene que desperdiciarla en

           trabajar para ganarse la vida?" - Mafalda

           "If past looks good and approach looks great, you can be quite confident about what price is telling you." - IF

           First off, I'm catching up, so my post is referring to your setup from here:

           HOME  EDUCATION  FORUM                                                           MORE  LOGOUT
           readthemarket.com/index.php/forum/journa...urse?start=300#30231

           So, I've been trying to get my head around why you choose the SR (blue line on 2nd
           chart) that you do, and consider it still valid after price has swung as many times
           through it, as it has. Trying to trade from that SR each way is going to ruin your day (or
           weeks, in this case) so all I can see is that you wait until price creates a tiny little flag on
           either side of the SR line, that you want to trade from. So, I suppose on your charts you
           note the poles, mark the SR line, and wait, and wait, and maybe wait, until the teeny
           flag gets created way off in the future, and then wait some more to see and FTR
           created after that. Then, guess what, more waiting! But that's ok, as you can go digging
           in the past, and just set alerts, so you sort of time-warp. So, I've gone looking for
           another example, and perhaps you can tell me if I'm on the right lines or not. Looking at
           my first chart, my area of interest is the potential QM forming, but that's just to show
           where I'm looking, really. In any case, it's a major turning point, so a good place to go
           looking for long entries.

           DrSwing wrote:
           My thoughts on these CPs is that they are really flags in a trend. They allow
           momentum to build up for a further push higher. These shallow CP flags can
           allow a trend channel to continue for quite some time as price doesn't get
           overstretched. They can provide an opportunity to join a move that is already
           under way. I guess the CP that is really of value is where price compresses into
           a strong SD zone, so you get in at the start of a move with the Big Boys and a
           chance of good R:R. The CP into the zone can be an indication that the SD zone
           will be a strong level.

           DocS

           Dr. Swing,

           So how do you know where one flag ends and the other begins? Also, how do you

           know how to use this with HTF charts?

           DocS

           Dr. Swing,

           So how do you know where one ag ends and the other begins? Also, how do you
           know how to use this with HTF charts?

           Thanks,
           H

           Hi Hotochin, thank you for giving me the opportunity to compound my understanding by
           explaining how I read the charts.

           First of all as we often read "trading is more of an art than a science". I guess the
           general explanation of a concept is the science and how WE interpret it is the art. Like
           building a house, the foundation, walls, windows, doors and roof is the basic concept
           (science) of a house but how it is designed, built and decorated is the art.

           I just marked up the current M15 chart of EUR/USD and how I would read it in terms of
           flags. I didn't want to search for perfect cleans examples as that is not what we meet

https://www.readthemarket.com/index.php/en/forum/journals/1640-drswing-on-course?start=25                6/38
7/27/2018                                  DrSwing on course - Page 2 - RTM Community

           everyday in our trading. Also the pole areas between the flags extended to the right, I

                   HOME      EDUCATION     FORUM                                           MORE  LOGOUT

           would call PAZs.

           I would use the HTFs to see where these flags and poles are located as price moves
           from demand origin to supply origin. A bit like zooming out on a google map to see
           where Hole 3 is located on the golf course :) .

           I'll get to your other question shortly.

           thx again
           drswing

           ok, this makes sense to me. So I get that price likes to move from one consolidation
           zone to the next. I take it you must look at higher tim frame S/D and then look for what
           happens to these zones in lower time frames? In other words if price is approaching a
           60min time frame, you would look to 15 or 5 to see how price reacts to those zones. If
           on the lower time frames the zones that were made get engulfed on the way up, you
           then look to short.

              from one consolidation zone to the next. I take it you must look at higher tim
              frame S/D and then look for what happens to these zones in lower time frames?
              In other words if price is approaching a 60min time frame, you would look to 15
              or 5 to see how price reacts to those zones. If on the lower time frames the
              zones that were made get engulfed on the way up, you then look to short.

           Not so sure about the bit I've marked in red!! You may have meant to say "If on the
           lower time frames the zones that were made on the way up get engulfed, you then look
           to short." Basically, when I have HTF SD and price is in and around that SD zone, I look
           to the LTFs for the engulf of a significant LTF SD and then a retrace to a DP to enter.
           Pretty much as charted above in post #32.

           Hey DrSwing,

           I can relate so much with how you are feeling. I have done 2 years worth of studying
           and just a little bit of trading. I usually quit trading to get back to learning because I can
           tell I am not ready, which I believe is the right thing to do. But at some point you have to
           take the plunge. Now Im not saying I know everything perfectly thats taught here, but I
           have most of it down pretty good, and believe that I can at least be profitable. I may not
           double my account in a month, but I can no longer make the excuse that I haven't
           learned enough yet. I have felt that way since about the beginning of the year. But I still
           found it hard to really dive in and accept that I do know this stuff.

           So we know that trading is a game of probabilities. Its easy to say that and even know
           it, but until you experience it, it is hard to put your hard earned money on the line,
           especially after a few losses in a row. Some people say use a demo account to learn
           which I agree with when it comes to learning methodology. But a game of probabilities
           requires a certain mindset and control that is hard to get a feel for on a demo account.
           And really trading itself isn't ideal for getting a feel for these things. With as many
           variables that are in play, it can be very hard to know if you caused your loss cause you
           missed something, or if the law of probabilities struck you.

           I went to a casino recently. I am not a gambler and usually just go to get away, and
           might play some slots. But for some reason I was really drawn to the blackjack table.
           Never sat at one before, and when I sat at this one I just fessed up and said this is my
           first time ever sitting at a table at a casino. Everyone was very polite and helped me as

https://www.readthemarket.com/index.php/en/forum/journals/1640-drswing-on-course?start=25                  27/38
7/27/2018                               DrSwing on course - Page 2 - RTM Community

           much as they could. But I was opened up to a new world when a guy at the table

           HOME  EDUCATION              FORUM                                              MORE  LOGOUT
           offered me his cheat sheet.

           I got home and got on the internet to study blackjack. I had no idea the amount of work
           and scientific study that had gone into the game of blackjack. The cheat sheet tells you
           the highest probability action to take in any situation. Its called basic strategy and will
           reduce the houses edge in blackjack down to 50.5% in the long run. I have since
           memorized this chart and can say without a doubt I can make the highest probability
           play no matter what is showing on the table. The beauty of this is that it requires no
           analysis and has no emotional attachment. The only variable is the card thats going to
           get turned over, and it either goes your way or it doesnt. It turns blackjack into a pure
           probability game, and you can sit and experience the ebbs and flows that are gonna
           happen.

           Now the whole problem with blackjack at a casino, is that even armed with basic
           strategy you are still no better than betting on a coin flip. Learning how to count cards
           gives you an extra 1.5%, so it flips the edge over to you. And even if the dealer turned
           over his other card and showed it to you, you are still only going to get to a 60% edge.
           The reason is because you are forced to place a bet before the cards are dealt and you
           must play whatever you are dealt. That and you have no idea what the next card is
           going to be. If you could sit back and play only the hands that the dealer shows a 6,
           then you would rack up everytime you went to the casino. But even then you would
           have losing hands, so you still must bet the same everyhand.

           What is so great about trading is you don't have to play every hand. You can sit out the
           hands when the dealer is showing a 10 card to your 16. And the tools that IF and the
           gang, and the guys at AG have given us allows us to deal ourselves a 20 everytime, but
           even then the dealer can still get 21.

           Good Luck DrSwing. And may the odds be forever in your favor.....

           smimon                   Offline Trader                                                      Posts: 549

                                                      Thank you received: 1075

              keen246 wrote: I just finished this journal. It was indeed a great learning
              experience. I just can help wondering why people who are doing great on their
              threads/journals suddenly disappear without a trace. Is it because they became
              so proficient and no longer feel the need to discuss on the forums? Is it because
              they realized making money on Forex is too hard and stop trying? Is it because
              they concluded the markets are not for them? Is it because...?

           Not to wish to speak on behalf of DrSwing, but I believe it's because documenting your
           thoughts and trying to teach others is a cathartic part of one's own learning process.

           Once you feel near to attaining a level of mastery, the urge to journal everything and
           constantly comment on the posts of others evaporates somewhat. I presume the
           majority of good traders on here who were writing regularly updated journals, and then
           stopped, simply reached the natural end of their journey, and lost the compulsion to
           write updates any more. Perhaps some went on to other things - but I'm not sure how
           many people would, after devoting months and years to learning a very difficult
           discipline which is essentially a license to print money, would totally stop doing it,
           unless they were simply in it for the intellectual challenge.

           The following user(s) said Thank You: Ifmyante, cuimingzhao

           opportunities to join the ride down.

           I had a question if you dont mind. I was looking at this trade and I see two things that

           HOME                   EDUCATION  FORUM                                         MORE  LOGOUT
           would have been a consideration. The price at which you shorted, which is 1.30837,

           assuming you shorted at the high of that entry bar - i am assuming this is the circled

           high on that first chart which you show in the inset as a RBD. On my charts I see this

           price level matching that of a bar at 20:00 on 4/21. I am trying to pinpoint what the

           actual supply level is, as I see the real next supply level being closer to the base area of

           that large down move on Wednesday. Assuming this mentioned price level was the

           perceived supply level, I given this is a compressed down move I would have been

           wary of price coming back up. That might have circumvented me even taking the trade

           actually. But assuming I did, I can see that there are two bars on Friday - 12:00 AM and

           4:AM on the H4 chart, with the next one having a spike into that base area I mentioned

           of the large down move on Wednesday. I would have been looking at this area around

           here as the zone, and perhaps I would have risked putting my stop at least at the open

           of the 4AM bar on Friday, since that is where price begins to move away from. This

           would cause me to have to risk around 10-15 points The target would be the low of the

           Wednesday bar, which according to my calcs is around 845 away from the entry point.

           Theres clearly another level not that far below too which would have been a nice

           second target for me.

           I am no pro by any means so please take my views as amateur at best. I guess my own
           take away from this is that the better trade was the pullback on Friday in the 8:00 AM
           bar, since this was a return back up into supply after the first engulf. And I would have
           factored n the R:R, and either simply not traded, traded for a scalp, or placed two
           targets that I mentioned with my stop a little closer - still not the 25 pips you mentioned
           though; half that.

           I do like the fact price reacted down where it did, which to me feels like it is right at the
           edge of that supply level, warranting that stop to be at least higher. I think you are right,
           price had capped, but I see this as a rejection fro the edge of the supply zone; I could
           very well be reading this all wrong too.

           Sorry theres no chart - I am not on my main computer so I hope what I write here isnt
           completely useless.

           All the best,

Hi DrSwing,

I uploaded an image which I hope is a little clearly than my original verbiage. In thinking
more about your trade, on H4, I think the trade was pretty good and the unfortunate
thing was stop placement. I didnt feel like I could comfortably apprehend the supply
above your entry safely, so I (rightly or wrongly) would have been looking to get out
lower down around the demand level (I show this area in the chart).

My common tactic is to trade the first pullback, and then scalp or short swing the move
down. The area around the entry had been a little choppy and I cannot see any strong
moves away that would suggest theres much more supply left there. Thats why I would
have been rating a retest of the entry bars high with a low figure for holding, especially
because breakout traders would be above that bars high, as well as shorters who were
hurting fro shorting lower down.

I am learning too, so take that into account when reading my own thoughts on this
trade, which is all they are. I spent some time reading your journal today and it is clear I
can learn a lot from your posts. This trade could very well be one of those many trades
that just dont work because they just didnt work; thats it. Risk management around the
stops is something I have had a few issues with if I am honest. So I am always looking

https://www.readthemarket.com/index.php/en/forum/journals/1640-drswing-on-course?start=50                               11/31
7/27/2018                               DrSwing on course - Page 3 - RTM Community

           to the ends of the bars to think about what might going on when the order book hits that

               HOME       EDUCATION     FORUM                                              MORE  LOGOUT
           level. And I often place my stop according to that, and if I dont see the target giving me

           the right R:R I just pass on the trade or definitely ensure I can scalp. Assuming a

           standard lot trade size thats not a bad trade to the first target.

           Thanks again,

           MF

           DrSwing on course                                                                             #68

                        DrSwing                             Offline Master Trader

                        Posts: 419 Thank you received: 1084

           I had a trade last week (posted in my journal) where I got stopped out (faked out) by
           5pips before price went in my direction. Price then just smashed back down without any
           retrace to allow me to re-enter the move. On post analysis I was happy with the setup
           and just put it down to a trade that didn't work, and moved on ........... Or so I had
           thought, but I found myself thinking that this could happen again on my next swing,
           since I didn't have a reason as to why it had happened!! We are usually scared of
           things that we don't understand. Knowing that this would lead to indecision and
           hesitancy in my future shot making, I set about analysing FOs (fake outs).

           Just for clarity, I'll be talking about:

           S/D - Supply/Demand
           SUP - Supply
           D - Demand
           S/R - Support Resistance
           Swap - Suport becomes resistance and vice versa
           ENG - Engulf
           FTR - Failure To Return
           FTB - First Time Back
           FO - Fake Out

https://www.readthemarket.com/index.php/en/forum/journals/1640-drswing-on-course?start=50                     22/31
7/27/2018                               DrSwing on course - Page 3 - RTM Community

         FL - Flag Limit  EDUCATION     FORUM                                              MORE  LOGOUT

                 HOME

Using a Weekly chart of the AUD/USD I've marked one FO pattern in red and the other
FO in blue. I've kept all markings on the chart to a minimum for clarity. Basically I'm
saying here that the area between an FTR and the price zone that it failed to returned
to, is a potential FO zone. This FO zone needs to be narrow to have potential, as the
pros have to be able to drive price beyond the FTR to the S/D level.

To me an FTR can relate to any important zone, whether it be S/D, S/R, Swap or FL.
The red pattern has an S/R - FTR FO zone and the blue pattern has an S/D - FTR FO
zone. FOs provide large liquidity for the pros to buy/sell into and so they can lead to a
large move in price. Coast to coast type of thing.

The following user(s) said Thank You: Harry, pucci, geejay, windorz, Zaeneix, cuimingzhao

           It was a nice bulltrap. The support level around 3144 was basically already taken out
           after the spike below, so the move up into the supply area provided low risk entries for
           the big players, and huge rewards. As you marked, this was a multiple R:R trade if
           taken at "real" supply.

           DrSwing, you could have taken the entry off lower timeframes after we engulfed the
           "base" from the HTF supply retest at S/R flip. It looks scary to enter short there because
           of the previous "bull bar", but in fact, there was no buy level because of the spike down
           clearing path for further move.

           Take a look at this stretched EURUSD analysis (m5 chart) - you have to right click and
           expand to read comments

           So I've been taking a look at price action around news, and with NFP (non farm payroll)
           out today it was the perfect test bed. The first chart were my thoughts on the EUR/USD
           well before news time. This is pretty much how I view trades. After the engulf south,
           where is price going to retrace to, so that I can enter a short? But NFP adds a little
           spice to things!! Now I didn't trade this, I just made a plan and watched how it unfolded
           or unravelled :) .

           HOME  EDUCATION                    FORUM                                        MORE  LOGOUT

           Here is some TP places on your chart. What I would say to you is, follow the trade on
           H1 and keep marking the swing lows on the way up. Moment you get an engulf of
           swing low, you know, it is time to get out. This would keep you in the trade for maximum
           time while giving you good time to react once you see an engulf. Checking for engulf on
           LTFs would scare you unnecessarily.

         Good luck man. Nice journal.                                                                    24/34

Ramjet                                    Offline Trader                                          Posts: 56

                                      Thank you received: 103

Hi ya H..

Hope you don't mind me stepping in to answer part of your question

   I am thinking that it is a good idea to shrink up the chart to find the PAZ's right?

I feel this is one thing a lot of traders tend to miss out on as I have only come to realize
only over the last few months....From the chart example I've attached here of charts
side by side, one zoomed 1x extra the other not you will see how you can easily miss
important levels on the chart..this works with congestion zones, Trend Lines, PAZ, etc..

It's a bit like the old saying I think it's "forrest from the trees" ...You've gotta see the big
picture..
Just like zooming from the higher TF's to the lower TF's(if you like the lower TF's) for
tighter stops and entries from the higher TF trend and PA..

Cheers,
Adrian

https://www.readthemarket.com/index.php/en/forum/journals/1640-drswing-on-course?start=100                      10/42

           There are no Coincidences! Posts: 77 Thank you received: 114

           Eagerly waiting for further explanations from you regarding PAZ .

           To be honest, this explanation quoted below from your post in Classroom thread is by

           far the clearest I've come across that has helped me better to understood about

           marking PAZ  .

           "The level at which price leaves a zone and fails to get back into the zone is the
           FTR (FL). So we now have a PAZ above the FTR and another PAZ below the
           FTR, and we can operate within these individual zones"

           Really a eye opener when viewed with this clear pictorial description by IF.

           Hey Doc excellent chart. It got me wondering why it started breaking down up there so I
           started digging.

https://www.readthemarket.com/index.php/en/forum/journals/1640-drswing-on-course?start=100                21/42
7/27/2018                              DrSwing on course - Page 5 - RTM Community

           I believe the opposing FTRs cause the highs (of price, not the drawn boxes) of the

           HOME          EDUCATION     FORUM                                                MORE  LOGOUT
           current PAZ, and if the current highs of the PAZ engulfed the opposing FTRs then price

           should have no problem jumping up to the next PAZ. Especially if price comes back

           down to a level/ftr smoothly without basing any, then it should be an easy ride up.

           Its when price does things in extremes that makes all this hard. Like when supply and
           demand are close together the s/r lines are severly blurred (or you could say the PAZs
           are layered on top of each other), or like here when you have so much upward
           momentum the s/r lines are stretched far apart and barely visible. You have to go down
           to a smaller TF to see the effect. It all works exactly the same, it just makes it harder to
           pull the trigger. But if we can understand the extremes, then the middle ground should
           be cake.

           edit: Excuse the bad grammar in the chart, also I wasn't trying to show anything with
           that bottom white line on my chart. I was actually looking to see how price reacted to
           that ftr on the way up and accidentally left it on the chart. There was so much demand,
           or should I say manipulation, the supply hardly made a dent.

           I agree with Hotochin. That level is reacting to demand that had made a higher high and

           case. Even though my idea of a PAZ on this chart is very small, that would just be

           being blurred or offset and the PAZs being layered. Which usually means compression.
           And I think thats what this little sequence here is, a bit of compression spread out over

           Dr SwinHg,OSMir.EI read thEisDpUoCsAt oTfIyOoNurs abouFtOaRmUoMnth ago, ManOdRitEdidn't mLeaOnGaOwUhTole

           lot to me. Now I've started on your thread again I'd like to let you know that this is pure
           gold for me. I've been furrowing my brow regarding the depth a FO can go, and this has
           been immensely helpful in placing together my disparate (desperate, too) thoughts.

           Cheers.

           DrSwing wrote: I had a trade last week (posted in my journal) where I got
           stopped out (faked out) by 5pips before price went in my direction. Price then
           just smashed back down without any retrace to allow me to re-enter the move.
           On post analysis I was happy with the setup and just put it down to a trade that
           didn't work, and moved on ........... Or so I had thought, but I found myself
           thinking that this could happen again on my next swing, since I didn't have a
           reason as to why it had happened!! We are usually scared of things that we don't
           understand. Knowing that this would lead to indecision and hesitancy in my
           future shot making, I set about analysing FOs (fake outs).

           Just for clarity, I'll be talking about:

           S/D - Supply/Demand
           SUP - Supply
           D - Demand
           S/R - Support Resistance
           Swap - Suport becomes resistance and vice versa
           ENG - Engulf
           FTR - Failure To Return
           FTB - First Time Back
           FO - Fake Out
           FL - Flag Limit

             Using a Weekly chart of the AUD/USD I've marked one FO pattern in red and the                           38/42

https://www.readthemarket.com/index.php/en/forum/journals/1640-drswing-on-course?start=100
7/27/2018                             DrSwing on course - Page 5 - RTM Community

           other FO in blue. I've kept all markings on the chart to a minimum for clarity.
           HOME         EDUCATION                            FORUM                          MORE  LOGOUT
           Basically I'm saying here that the area between an FTR and the price zone that it

           failed to returned to, is a potential FO zone. This FO zone needs to be narrow to

           have potential, as the pros have to be able to drive price beyond the FTR to the

           S/D level.

           To me an FTR can relate to any important zone, whether it be S/D, S/R, Swap or
           FL. The red pattern has an S/R - FTR FO zone and the blue pattern has an S/D -
           FTR FO zone. FOs provide large liquidity for the pros to buy/sell into and so they
           can lead to a large move in price. Coast to coast type of thing.

           The following user(s) said Thank You: kutumbaba, cuimingzhao

           Hotochin, I hope all goes well for your dad, and I guess it's good to have this forum as a
           distraction while in ICU.

           I see the market as having zones within zones within zones, and so FTRs can be
           located in many areas on a chart. On this M5 chart there is so much CP on show that I
           am just not interested until price moves away from there, and hence why I posted the
           M15 chart to get a clearer view of where the edges of price can be found.

           I really need price patterns to scream at me, and if I have to look closely at a chart to

           determine what is happening, then I need to move up a tf or two or move to a different

           pair altogether. My viewing of a chart is usually done on the H4 tf or higher to determine

           my areas of interest, and only then do I move to the LTFs to pin point my entry. I see no

           value in wrecking my head  trying to decipher what is happening solely on an M5

           chart. As rcmacf stated, the simpler the better.

at which price flags and fails to return to the CP. This becomes the FTR and the edge of

           You just have to know that a compression itself is a PAZ (Price Action Zone)

           Then you have to look where its decision was made to break it (e.g by liq spike) - you
           can also refer to it as decision point.

           Look at your chart at the first liq spike from that cp. We tested it one time after the
           break, but then created a nice bullish momentum bar which broke this "FTR"

           DrSwing wrote:

           Hey Dr. Swing, how did you anticipate the FO? I noticed you wanted to take the
           trade above the source of the engulf. Or am I interpreting your charts wrong?

           Thanks,
           H

           H, I've got myself into the habit lately of taking screen shots during the day, of scenarios
           I think might play out. Then I can understand the story of price better as events unfold. I
           find that like a storybook, once I get a handle on the storyline my anticipation of events
           is much sharper. I really NEED to do this or my thinking is all over the place, and I
           forget what I had thought, and I continue to frustrate myself with missed opportunities.

           I will now only follow a chart, where I can get into the story. If I can't get a grasp of the
           storyline, then I close that chart and move on to the next book. I then must record my
           thinking to keep a track on the stories that I am following. A simple screen shot at the
           right place can do that for me, and I can then visualise the next chapter of the price

https://www.readthemarket.com/index.php/en/forum/journals/1640-drswing-on-course?start=150                22/50
7/27/2018                               DrSwing on course - Page 7 - RTM Community

           story. Many times the authors of price will have a different storyline than I had

           HOME         EDUCATION       FORUM                                               MORE  LOGOUT
           anticipated, but I just keep reading and I'm soon back following again.

           After all that, not sure if it's answered your question, H!! :) But I find the best chapters to
           help write, are the FO chapters, as they move very quickly with the plot, with little or no
           waiting around. So I keep a keen eye out for those.

           The following user(s) said Thank You: ZeeDeveel, cuimingzhao

           DrSwing-

           Its hard to reason about this without more TF charts etc = what is the symbol? It
           remains, abstractly, still possible the bullish move is a ruse and there is supply above
           here at the edge of where price made a decision to drop down. Remember there is
           going to be some demand here because of the trapped shorts that got stuck on the left
           and missed getting out. This gives the illusion of strength on the right here. Theres CP
           as the left side rises before dropping. There is a clear engulf higher up too, before the
           basing on the right here. The wick on that candle near the top, with some of the wicks
           as price slides to this base right above the H, would have been making it better for the
           demand to take price slightly higher, but I also notice the base of the lowest tail on the
           right - thats right at the pivot high on the left - right where breakout traders are.....

           Damned if I do, damned if I don't. Posts: 95 Thank you received: 219

           I'd say it's a quick LQ-retest of the SR level, because, what has just happened is
           demand turned supply ( the circled area)

https://www.readthemarket.com/index.php/en/forum/journals/1640-drswing-on-course?start=175                      26/33
7/27/2018        DrSwing on course - Page 8 - RTM Community

           HOME  EDUCATION  FORUM                                                           MORE  LOGOUT

           Obviously sellers wanted to remove the demand from where the upward compression
           started,
           and even then it still drops in a cp fashion, probably just to make sure their stop loss is
           hit, so def stoploss hunt,
           maybe that's why the LQ followed, I'm not sure?

           Anyway, with this taken care off and price approaching this fresh supply level in such a
           strong fashion, yet gets rejected in an even stronger fashion, I reckon this was the last
           stand for the buyers, they used everything in their power to protect their level, but failed
           dramaticly.
           So result, a massive sell off.

           Approaching a fresh level like this and still holding doesn't make logic sense to me,
           must be manipulated.
           But ofcourse it happens from time to time, good to bring this up.
           Don't really have a name for it.

           HotRod                               Offline Trader                                    Posts: 108

                                                       Thank you received: 189

           Here is my reasoning for the large move back up to the top s/r flip. Nothing fancy, you
           guys already explained a lot of it.

           Disregard the rectangle around the demand and the arrow pointing to the wick, I have a
           question on that. Extremerrage mentioned he would have waited till price reached that
           top flag to take a trade. At first I saw the big wick and thought no way because it looked
           consumed, but Extremerrage knows what he is doing so it made me look further. Shorts
           were being built in that base. Usually when we say orders are consumed its when price
           re-enters a base after its left it. But in this case it looks like the wick was made while the
           shorts were still being entered into the market. When that bar started out it was in
           demand and looked very bullish, and probably filled their last orders at the top before
           they decided to break the flag to the south, leaving the wick behind. So in this case it
           seems the wick doesn't show consumption, but more shorts. Is this how you read it
           Extremerrage, and why you felt safe shorting there?

https://www.readthemarket.com/index.php/en/forum/journals/1640-drswing-on-course?start=175                      29/33

           I now mostly trade the HTFs for a longer term hold and using the LTFs for entries, but
           sometimes I will scalp the LTFs. On Friday I spotted a scalp setup, and as I often do, I
           took screenshots at varies intervals just to see how price played out. I have annotated
           the screenshots with my thinking at that time. You will not see any trades on the chart
           as I use it for analysis only and my scalping is done with a spread betting broker.

           I am posting these charts as I believe this is the essence of what RTM teaches at its
           simpliest. I have kept annotations to a minimum as I have difficulty reading cluttered
           charts and the real message can get lost to the reader. I shall return to this post should
           I ever stray from the path.
